counsel. - (pt) mr president, i thank you for giving me the opportunity to present the work of the united nations special session on the evaluation of the beijing conference and its action plan, which dates back to 1995. the beijing conference represented a very important milestone for the recognition of women's rights and the approval of an action plan signed by 189 states to give substance to these rights and to adopt instruments enabling their progressive realisation. as is customary for such international instruments, it was necessary to evaluate it 15 years after it had been signed by 189 states, as i recalled. this evaluation took place over many sessions, firstly through preparatory meetings within propcom, which gradually overcame some difficulties in terms of acquis on some aspects considered important by a group of countries, especially those usually considered to be the most developed, as well as in terms of resistance to the language used, not in the political declaration which was possible to approve earlier, but in the final document which has four parts, one of which is on the measures and policies to be adopted by governments in order to implement not only the beijing plan, but everything that the different countries had already acquired in the meantime. this process was relatively complex, with some countries, notably the european union, allied with latin american countries and the g77 showing some cracks at one point, which did not contribute to the rapid progress of the work. in any case, and to sum up as quickly as possible, we can say that there has been significant progress on: the recognition of women's rights as human rights, with very strong political statements to not accept that these human rights are violated; the eradication of all practices that violate human rights; the reference to the commission on the status of women, the optional protocol and the fight against racism and racial violence. as regards the issue of violence against women, i would highlight, among the many advances made, the following: the concertation on combating trafficking in women through international cooperation, with support for actions developed at other levels in the international field, and the acceptance by all countries of the implementation of public campaigns against violence, on the basis of the zero-tolerance campaign led by the european union; poverty eradication policies; work as a means of ensuring women's economic participation; the adoption of policies for reconciling family and working life; the need to develop women's access to education and training as a fundamental instrument to guarantee their autonomy, and also, for the first time, the very strong reference to the issues of motherhood and fatherhood (the role of the man is always associated) and the need to share the tasks. in the area of health, it was found that a comprehensive approach was needed in terms of sexual rights, but not in the area of reproductive health. at the initiative of the european union, specific references were introduced for migrant disabled people, elderly women and indigenous people. as i have already said, there was a great deal of emphasis on the role of men and the need to help strengthen the role of women in building ever more peaceful societies; the introduction of something that has been one of the fundamental instruments of the beijing action plan, such as the mainstreaming of gender perspective, which will have to be included in all human rights mechanisms, un treaties and the millennium summit, was also discussed, which is an extraordinarily important achievement. as regards the methodologies for assessing progress in implementing the commitments made by states, it is also worth noting the existence of measurable and well-defined objectives, which make it possible to take a more objective assessment of what has been achieved at any time. there was also talk of strengthening the role of non-governmental organisations in the conduct of all policies and as preferred partners of the state, with a view to ensuring compliance with the plan. the main problems are strongly linked to what the european union already considers to be the content of article 13 of the treaty of amsterdam, to the difficulty of some countries in accepting the issue of sexual rights and the diversity it entails, and to sexual orientation. in this context, in order to follow the strategy defined at european union level to consider this special session not as a new conference but as an intermediate stage, we considered that the language of beijing should be taken up on the more delicate aspects, the european union deciding to make a final declaration in which it reaffirms the progress made and its intention to continue its battle so that what already corresponds to a commitment in terms of the constitution of the european union can be enshrined in future instruments, i refer specifically to article 13.
commission. - mr president, i am very pleased to be here today to present the commission's opinion on the conclusions of the extremely important conference on gender equality held last week in new york. i would like to apologise for the absence of my colleague, commissioner diamantopoulou, who was unable to be available this afternoon. as some of you may know, she is being held back by commitments she made before the date of this debate was set. as you also know, she is due to come to the committee next week to have a thorough exchange of views with the members who have contributed so much to the success of last week's conference. meps will be able to take advantage of commissioner diamantopoulou's visit to the committee next week to discuss this or that point with her. i should like to begin by thanking the portuguese presidency on behalf of the commission for coordinating the position of the member states so effectively and for handling the negotiations with the other delegations so competently, which, according to my information, took place in conditions which were sometimes extremely difficult. we must also thank the european parliament for approaching this special session in such an exceptional spirit of cooperation. the members of the committee on women's rights and equal opportunities who were part of the delegation made a very valuable contribution. but they also took the initiative to cooperate with members of the european parliament from the member states and associated countries. thank you for this outstanding picture of political coordination. we, the members of the commission, also welcome the motion for a resolution tabled by parliament: it is a firm and constructive statement of parliament's commitment to gender equality. the european union can be proud, because it was the most progressive regional group represented in new york. together we have fought hard to make progress against beijing. and we owe part of our success to the unity of the institutions. and, i think we can say, we did it! in some areas, the outcome far exceeds the result achieved five years ago in beijing - a conference i remember watching a little more closely than i would have today. we have achieved this without watered down the beijing platform in other areas. the document reaffirms that women's rights are human rights. it urged all un members to sign the optional protocol to the cedaw convention and called for the work of the international criminal court to give greater weight to the issue of sex discrimination. the document recognises that discrimination against women is often mixed with other types of discrimination. while we did not obtain any specific mention of the grounds listed in the ec treaty, the final statement of the presidency to the general assembly clearly reflects the position of the european union on this matter. the duty of the state to put an end to violence against women, including domestic violence, and to halt the appalling growth of trafficking in women is expressed in new and much clearer language. the outcome document links gender equality with improved economic prosperity. if women are given the opportunity to participate fully in economic and social life, they will be better able to lift themselves and their communities out of poverty. it seems inconceivable to me, given my responsibilities in this area, to have a model of sustainable economic development which does not take full account of the issue of gender equality. the document reaffirms the importance of education as a means of empowering girls and women. it also refers to the need to ensure a fair distribution of work between men and women, including in household work, and to take measures to reconcile work and family life. it also discusses methodology - in other words, how to ensure that these good intentions are put into practice. under pressure from the european union, the document recognises the importance of establishing benchmarks and indicators of progress. finally, we welcome the fact that the document refers to the role that men can play in gender equality, given the considerable responsibility they have in this regard. the union has never been better equipped to deliver on the commitments made in beijing, thanks to its stronger stance on human rights, gender equality and discrimination. the treaty of amsterdam extends the union's scope of action to allow it to advance the implementation of the beijing platform for action. i hope that no one doubts that this is precisely the commission's objective. it is my colleague, commissioner diamantopoulou, who is doing all this, but it is also the desire of all the members of the commission to be fully involved in this work.

counsel. - (pt) as you know, the european union's position on arms exports is set out in the code of conduct adopted in june 1998. among the criteria set out in this code, respect for human rights is one of the main conditions to be met for authorisation to be granted. however, decisions are taken individually by each member state, which must follow - it is a matter of good political faith - the provisions of the code of conduct. the council receives no information on the reasons for which member states grant or refuse export licences. it cannot therefore pass judgment on the basis of that decision. i would like to say, however, that there is beginning to be, within the framework of the european union and particularly after the establishment of the code of conduct, a set of exchanges of information on arms exports which is the subject of an annual report and which will obviously begin to create common forms of approach to the problems. however, we must remember that this question, which is always based on the existence of the code of conduct, remains a purely subsidiary issue, to be decided at the level of each member state.

council. - (pt) i was surprised that there was no question on this subject, and i am therefore glad that it has finally been put. the council of ministers of the european union does not have to take a decision on a decision taken by 14 member states in the context of their bilateral relations with austria. this is not a matter for the council of the european union. the situation would be different if, for example, austria, by failing to comply with an aspect of the treaty on european union, were to infringe the provisions of article 6 and were therefore to be punished or justify the application of article 7. as this is not the case, what is happening today in the context of the relations between the 14 countries and austria remains a matter of the bilateral sphere, for which the council has no competence.
- (pt) mr president, the council is composed of 15 countries and it does not appear to me that austria has participated in any debate or vote which has led to any action being taken within the council with regard to austria. no action has been taken against austria by any eu body. i would even add that during the six months of the portuguese presidency, austria did not have the opportunity, like this parliament, to participate fully and actively in all the workings of the union in any of the institutions of the european union. the bilateral decisions taken by the 14 countries against austria - i am not talking about sanctions - are a legitimate political reading for any country vis-à-vis another country. every country has the right, in relation to the evolution of the political situation in another country, to adopt a position and draw the necessary consequences. let me remind you of the consequences of this. these include the lack of support for austrian candidates in international organisations, the fact that austrian ambassadors are not received at a level higher than that of a director-general, and the freezing of visits of a political nature. these are the so-called "sanctions". the fact that some people have been able to read extensively about this type of behaviour is their own business, which is why these "sanctions", as you call them, are bilateral in nature, everyone does what he likes and the responsibility is collective. the responsibility for the position adopted by the 14 countries has been freely assumed by the 14 countries. each of them is free to withdraw from these measures and we must also ask why, to date, none of the 14 countries has withdrawn from the measures that have been taken. this question does not therefore concern the council, it is a question which the austrian authorities have had the opportunity to raise within the framework of the european union: it was raised by the austrian foreign minister at the informal meeting of foreign ministers, it was raised the day before yesterday by the austrian foreign minister in luxembourg and it is very likely that mr wolfgang schüssel will take the opportunity offered by his stay in santa maria da feira to raise it, but this is not a community decision, it is a bilateral decision, whether you like it or not.

the rapporteur. - (nl) mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the commission has certainly shown by this communication that it is prepared to make a real contribution to improving the situation of victims of crime. it noted that in an area of freedom, security and justice within the european union, citizens must be able to enjoy effective legal protection. the commission also intends to open a debate on the treatment of victims and wants measures to be taken to improve the situation of victims. the committee on civil liberties, justice and home affairs feels that a number of the proposed measures are too dependent on the goodwill of each member state. it insists that regulation be adopted at european level. why is this one so indispensable? the last few years have given us countless examples of how victims are treated: children are abducted, parents, family members are victims, there are also victims of terrorist attacks, victims of organized crime, burglaries, armed attacks and so on, harassment, intimidation. victims cannot be wronged twice, first as victims of a crime and then as victims of the system. the rights of victims and transparency in their situation must be clarified. the measures proposed in the commission's communication appear to be exclusively focused on victims from a member state or third-country victims legally residing in a member state. however, when establishing a package of measures, account should be taken of victims from member states in third countries and of victims from third countries who are illegally resident in a member state. the status of a victim should not have any influence on his or her rights and the possibilities of enjoying those rights. in the context of the enlargement of the european union, special attention must be paid to the treatment of victims in the candidate countries. the treaties provide the european union with the means of action to strengthen the protection of victims of crime within its territory, both in civil and criminal proceedings. it is indeed regrettable that the unanimity rule should apply in this case. the scoreboard presented by the commission to assess progress in the establishment of the area of freedom, security and justice should be specified in such a way as to ensure and accelerate the legislative follow-up of the directive in question. it is also worrying to note that in this table, measures to help victims of crime are not foreseen before 2004. we cannot leave the victims waiting until that date. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment. proper and dignified treatment of victims also plays a preventive role. treating the victim helps to avoid stigmatization and enhances the role of the messenger. in the context of assistance to victims, it is important not to limit ourselves to financial and legal assistance but to adopt a series of measures guaranteeing anonymity, respect for the privacy and safety of victims, respect for cultural and religious aspects, recognition of the family of the victim as victims and witnesses themselves, the right to be assisted by an interpreter and to be questioned in their mother tongue, and the provision of medical, psychological and social assistance. it also sets out a specific system to provide legal, psychological and physical protection for victims of domestic violence, harassment and sexual mutilation. in particular, additional measures must be taken in respect of minors. special hearing rooms should be provided to record their testimony on video, so as to avoid a further confrontation with the traumatic experience. the material, medical, psychological and social assistance must be provided immediately and free of charge to the victim at the time of the offer. information on the availability of different forms of assistance should be provided to victims. it is extremely important that specialised help be provided according to the nature of the crime. in this respect, a number of prejudices need to be removed, including the fact that certain forms of crime are given different weight depending on the country. in addition, it was found that discrimination was practised in the treatment of victims. member states should be required to set up special police units for victims of crime. another essential point is the existence of a legal basis and the harmonization of the protection measures applicable to victims. the study carried out by an wergens as part of the grotius programme contains all the basic information needed on this subject. the experience gained in some member states can play an important role in harmonising measures or establishing a common set of measures. this kind of initiative is a first step on the road to prevention. however, given the increase in crossborder organised crime, the initiative should lead to a common prevention programme at european and international level. pending the establishment of a harmonised instrument for the cross-border protection of victims, member states and candidate countries should work towards improving the protection of victims, in collaboration with ngos. in the context of crime prevention, it is important to take into account the inherent security aspects of urban development and spatial planning.
- (nl) mr president, i have already received a number of reactions from the world of smes to the text we are going to approve tomorrow; i would like to congratulate the rapporteur on his work, and i hope that mr linkohr will convey this compliment to our colleague mr murphy. i would also like to thank the commission for its constructive and unwavering cooperation. the joint text which we shall be approving tomorrow and which we were able to approve in the conciliation committee is worth looking at. it regulates what needs to be regulated. no more, no less than that. it regulates matters in so far as they require european regulation, nothing more and nothing less, i repeat. it also regulates these matters in a well-organized legal manner. we can defend it in every respect, both in relation to transactions between traders and transactions between traders and the authorities. mr president, as early as 1993, when the internal market was just being born, parliament asked the commission to put forward a proposal to deal with late payments. the commission first took the easy way out by way of a recommendation and then took a more severe approach by submitting a proposal for binding rules. in two years we have done our best to formulate the necessary political choices and to formulate them correctly from a technical point of view. the roadmap has now been drawn up and the implementation is still to be carried out. member states also have two years to do so. we hope, together with the thousands of smes concerned, that the member states will respect this deadline for application, and even better, we encourage them to speed things up! i think, mr president, that a european medal can be awarded to the member state which completes its task first. i would like to ask the commission one more question. i would like to know whether it intends to make proposals on the conditions for setting up cash offices, a matter which we discussed at length during the preparations but which we have wisely omitted from this directive. the commission has already taken the necessary steps to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full.
commission - (pt) mr president, i would say that the commission is speaking at this stage of the debate simply to welcome and congratulate the european parliament and especially its rapporteur, mr simon francis murphy, on the very positive result achieved in the conciliation committee, which, after a hard negotiation, as all good negotiations do, has led to the adoption of this directive, which is, i am convinced, as other speakers have pointed out, a particularly important directive for european companies insofar as it concerns all commercial transactions between companies and between authorities, both in the public and private sectors. i think this is a response to a remark made by mr rolf linkohr. we hope and believe that this directive will have a positive impact on the day-to-day business transactions of all businesses, whether large multinational companies or smes. but it must be admitted that it is in the case of smes that a directive like this reveals its existential nature, because that is where the effectiveness of the directive on late payment will be most positive, under the rules of the functioning of the market. i am convinced that we are adopting a legal framework which is at the same time serious, rigorous and effective; the commission will do everything in its power not only to ensure the proper transposition of this directive into domestic law over the next two years, but also to undertake a possible reassessment of the situation of late payment in the light of its practical application. i would therefore like to conclude by once again congratulating mr simon francis murphy and parliament on the decision that will be taken.

- (nl) mr president, i do not have enough time to describe the fate of a country which has shown such an encouraging situation for so many years, but which has plunged in recent years - while the people of zimbabwe enjoy a good level of education - into an even greater stage of underdevelopment. this is probably one of the things we need to take advantage of in the early years of the mugabe government. the population is well aware of the situation, despite the fact that the opposition has been prevented from taking part in the normal electoral propaganda, at least in the official media. i know zimbabwe relatively well, having visited and followed its development for several years in a row, but i am nevertheless struck by the degree of intimidation that preceded the elections and i fear that this situation will continue even after the elections. i therefore believe that, in accordance with our international responsibility, we must take action and ensure that we do not lose the positive influence we had before the elections. and this is through difficult but concrete negotiations on the reforms that we intend to carry out in zimbabwe. the rural areas are being plagued by a regime of fear, which of course cannot last, because the quality of life of both whites and blacks is being degraded. i wanted to stress this because these people are drawn into a series of events over which they have no influence whatsoever. i hope that the dialogue with the government, the opposition and civil society can continue.
the council of ministers was deeply shocked by the dover affair and the death of 58 chinese nationals in tragic circumstances. this case reflects the increasing migratory pressure on western europe, to which we have been sensitive for some time. the marked strengthening of european solidarity is an indispensable response to a situation of extreme concern for our continent. first of all, it should be emphasized that the existence of organized channels and the expansion of the despicable but unfortunately lucrative trade of smuggling play a major role in illegal immigration, both in the increase in the flows themselves and in the means of passage, of which the dover tragedy provides an extreme illustration. it is known that the majority of illegal immigration reaches western europe through smugglers, especially for people from far-flung destinations such as china. this point must be emphasized in response to those who say that it is immigration control that will produce illegals. the increase in irregular immigration is, on the contrary, the result of the growing activity of the channels of illegal entry. it is clear that this activity is facilitated by the legislative or operational inadequacies of the immigration states and by everything that allows illegals to enter and remain in the territories in violation of the laws. but there is more: behind the smugglers, there are more and more mafia gangs. police officials have identified a russian mafia, exploiting sri lankans and chinese; a turkish mafia taking care of kurds, afghans or iranians. we also know that illegal immigrants from the balkans are being handled by the albanian mafia. the concentration of a large number of these smugglers in calais, the point of entry to england - more than 10 000 have been arrested since august 1999 - has demonstrated the particularly active nature of the smugglers: 400 have been arrested since august 1999. it is therefore necessary to make it clear that illegal immigration has become a real form of human trafficking, which as such is a part of crime, and even of large-scale organized crime. the trafficking of illegal immigrants is easier, more profitable and less risky than other forms of trafficking, such as drug trafficking, and is becoming a major issue for police and judicial cooperation. human trafficking for sexual exploitation is one of the most heinous aspects of this trade. at this level of organisation, it is no longer just a matter of fighting illegal immigration, but of fighting crime. the real culprits are the organizers of illegal entry, in whose hands the would-be immigrants are merely the toys of a scenario that can lead to tragedy. it is therefore against these smugglers that the fight must be waged in the first place. illegal immigration or human trafficking is a new form of slavery that our democratic societies cannot tolerate. we know what it costs, for example, for a chinese national to use the services of a smuggler: between eur 10 000 and eur 20 000 and years of hard labour to repay this debt, deprived of all rights, of all protection, in often appalling conditions and under constant threat. the demagogy of demanding a massive reopening of labour immigration or the automatic right of residence for all immigrants only fuels this dream of europe and contributes to throwing hundreds of thousands of people into the clutches of illegal immigration every year. the european union must not make the mistake, and it does not intend to make the mistake, of concentrating its repression on those who organise this trafficking in human beings, which is now among the most cowardly and odious. the council is determined to do everything possible to combat this odious traffic. european cooperation must be based on the means available to the states. it is clear that they have decided to take measures to combat this scourge effectively. since 1995, france has had a central office for the suppression of irregular immigration and illegal work, which has greatly expanded its actions to dismantle channels in the last two years. this has resulted in the breaking and stopping of some 15 cases each year. in the face of the reality of human trafficking and trafficking linked to illegal immigration, the french presidency therefore wishes to promote european solidarity, based on the conclusions of the tampere summit and on the following principles: co-development, integration of foreigners, the fight against illegal immigration, a common asian policy. the first point is therefore to take account of the situation in the countries of origin. how can we dissuade people from leaving their countries to go to europe under any conditions and thus often place their fate in the hands of slave-trading networks? the council must base its action on the guidelines adopted in tampere, and i quote: 'the european union needs a comprehensive approach to immigration which addresses the political aspects of human rights and development issues in countries and regions of origin and transit'. indeed, many factors explain migration movements: the demographic difference between the north and the south, the continuing increase in income and wealth inequalities between the richest and poorest countries, the instability of certain political situations, and the often false image that immigrants have of their future in europe. but illegal immigration and human trafficking are not inevitable. they can be fought at the cost of strong political will. in a world of profound turmoil, the long-term control of flows is achieved through policies of co-development and the stabilisation of the countries of origin of immigration. the co-development is precisely intended to link the policies in favour of the development of these countries to enable migration flows to be controlled. france will endeavour to give a new impetus to this approach to co-development, making use of its experience in this field. in particular, an agreement has already been signed with senegal and others are being negotiated with mali and morocco. two questions are asked: how can local projects be encouraged, particularly by aiding the training and settlement of foreigners wishing to return to their country of origin to contribute to their economic development, and how can this local development contribute to controlling flows by stabilising populations? reflection on these two themes will enrich the future work of the high level group on asylum and immigration, which is now working to implement the concrete measures of five action plans, notably concerning morocco and sri lanka. the implementation of this priority will be the subject of a seminar to be held in paris on 6 and 7 july, with the participation of experts and a number of ministers. the aim is to prepare the guiding text for the work of the european union, on the basis of some pilot experiments carried out with countries which have cooperated in this field and which have stable state structures, i am thinking in particular of morocco. the second major priority of the european union, as expressed in tampere, is the integration of legal aliens. this is important in the fight against human trafficking, which is fuelled by the marginalization and exclusion of a part of foreigners in the european union. integration requires a firm policy in favour of equal economic and social rights and a similarly firm fight against all forms of discrimination, particularly in relation to employment. the french presidency plans to organise a seminar on this subject in october and intends to present to the council a draft text on the harmonization of long-stay visas, which is an essential instrument of integration. access to the nationality of the member states is also a fundamental aspect of the integration of foreigners into europe, but it is known that each country retains its own rules in this respect. the third point of strength in tampere is the fight against the trafficking in human beings, which i mentioned earlier, and this aspect is obviously at the heart of the french presidency's concerns. the tampere council emphasized this priority, and i quote: 'the european council stresses the need to ensure at all stages a more effective management of migration flows. the council is determined to combat illegal immigration at its source, in particular by tackling those involved in trafficking in human beings. we call for closer cooperation between the border control services of the member states.' following the events in dover, the feira european council issued a fresh call for swift action by the european union. the council, i quote, 'condemned the criminal acts perpetrated by those who profit from trafficking in human beings and expressed the european union's determination to intensify cooperation to defeat this crossborder crime which has caused so many more deaths in europe'. the council called on the future french presidency and the commission to implement the tampere conclusions in this area as a matter of urgency. the french presidency has already proposed four initiatives to the council. firstly, a draft directive introducing sanctions against carriers who transport passengers in an irregular situation. the draft text thus extends the provisions of the schengen convention and aims to provide the union with a common system of sanctions. it reaffirmed the obligation on carriers to take all measures to ensure that the persons they carry have the necessary travel documents and, where appropriate, visas, and the obligation to re-route or take over the re-routing of aliens who are not admitted for the abovementioned reasons. it also provides for sanctions against those who have carried passengers without the required documents or visas. the proposed text provides for a minimum penalty of eur 2000. a second draft framework decision aims to strengthen the criminal law enforcement of aid for illegal entry or residence. again, in accordance with the provisions of the schengen convention, which require the states parties to the convention to provide for sanctions against anyone who helps illegal entry or residence, france has presented a draft framework decision aimed at harmonising national legislation as regards the definition of criminal offences. it is therefore an important text for strengthening the repression of human trafficking. thirdly, the french presidency has also presented a draft directive on cooperation in the field of removal. its purpose is to facilitate the implementation of an expulsion measure taken by a member state against an alien who is in an irregular situation, when that measure is taken by any other state which has arrested the alien on its territory. fourthly, and finally, the improvement of european solidarity in the field of control of flows and external border control is one of the priorities of the council of ministers. france, as the presidency, has put forward new proposals in this area, including an action plan which will be discussed in the european institutions. first of all, there is an urgent need to strengthen police cooperation in the exchange of information on irregular immigration flows and channels. the centralisation and cross-referencing of this data, its analysis and its exploitation must be part of the existing working group. it is particularly important, as the feira conclusions underline, to develop the role of europol in the repression of trafficking in human beings. the french presidency therefore intends to ask europol to present without delay an assessment of the actions undertaken in this field. the other objective in police cooperation is to strengthen the early warning system, which must become sufficiently effective to enable the member states to be informed and to react in a timely manner to phenomena related to illegal immigration. this system involves the designation of contact points between the member states. it instructed the council presidency to arrange for the necessary consultation for the implementation of measures adapted to the situation. the strengthening of this coordination also involves the establishment of a network of member states' liaison officers in the countries of origin of immigration, in order to improve situational awareness and control of immigration at source, in particular by checking travel documents at boarding at airports. all these projects to strengthen the fight against irregular immigration will be the subject of a seminar on the channels to be held in paris on 20 and 21 july, in a few days. the seminar will bring together high-level representatives of the member states, the countries of central and eastern europe, the united states, australia, canada and mexico. several ministers from the member states, heads of various services and of the member states' border police will take part in the symposium. i would also like to stress that one of the key points of tampere is the principle of harmonising the asylum laws of the member states. it is also an important tool in the fight against illegal immigration. we are seeing a sharp increase in asylum applications in most of the countries of the european union. this increase reflects the increase in irregular arrivals, as 80 to 90% of asylum applications are rejected by the various countries with which they were lodged. the right of asylum is frequently used by the sectors as a legal tool to facilitate the entry and stay of illegal immigrants. the difficulty of our task lies in the need to reconcile this fundamental right to asylum, which is recognized by international conventions, with the fight against fraud. i would refer again to the tampere summit, where the european council reaffirmed the importance which the union and the member states attach to full respect for the right to asylum. the summit conclusions say that this system should include in the short term a clear and operational method for determining the state responsible for examining an asylum application, common standards for a fair and efficient asylum procedure and common minimum conditions for the reception of asylum seekers. it is therefore essential to make progress in improving the functioning of the dublin convention, as regards the determination of the state responsible for examining an asylum application. france is closely following the commission's ongoing work. it would be a major step forward if it were established that the state which initiated the entry of a foreigner into the european union is in fact solely responsible for examining his application for asylum. the forthcoming introduction of the eurodac system, which allows the centralisation of asylum seeker fingerprints, should contribute to the fight against fraud involving multiple applications. the objective of a fair and efficient asylum procedure in europe must also be achieved rapidly. the conditions for the reception of asylum seekers must therefore be harmonised as a matter of urgency in order to limit internal flows within the territory of the european union and thus to achieve a more harmonious distribution between the member states. the french presidency has therefore presented a draft guideline text to facilitate the work of the commission and the preparation of the future directive. we hope to reach initial conclusions at the council meeting next december, so as to contribute to the preparation of the commission's draft directive, which is due for early 2001. the council is therefore determined to combat trafficking in human beings by all means necessary, in accordance with the tampere and feira conclusions. the commission's analysis is that the question of the ways and means of illegal entry is no longer a question of immigration alone, but also a question of the fight against organised crime. it is in this perspective that we must work and the french presidency wishes to mobilise all the means - national, community, intergovernmental - to achieve this.
commission mr president, first of all i would like to stress that the commission shares the emotion caused by the tragedy in dover, as president prodi expressed it at the feira council and as i myself had the opportunity to express it on behalf of the commission. we consider that these events illustrate in a particularly dramatic way the need for a common immigration policy. the commission therefore welcomes the invitation issued by the heads of state or government in feira to speed up the process. it is firmly committed to contributing to this, together with the french presidency. the commission recalls that the framework for such a policy was set by the tampere european council and that precise deadlines for its implementation were set both by the treaty of amsterdam and by the european council itself. to have lasting effects, such a policy must be part of the overall approach defined in tampere, which implies both more effective management of migration flows and the development of a partnership with countries of origin and transit, the establishment of a common asylum system and the ensuring of fair treatment for third-country nationals legally residing in the territory of the member states. it is in this overall framework that the effort to be made to control the particularly heinous forms of crime, trafficking in human beings and exploitation, must be seen. they are the cause of the dover tragedy, but also of all the anonymous tragedies that happen every day without our knowing because the press does not report them. unfortunately, they do exist. in particular, the commission recalls that a very short deadline has been set for the council to adopt the necessary legislative provisions to be able to criminalize and sanction the behaviour of those who profit from human trafficking activities. a time limit has been set; the european union is also working to ensure that the eu's external borders are not encroached upon by the countries of the region. the commission confirms its willingness to cooperate with the council to ensure that this deadline is met. it notes that the french presidency is about to present a first initiative, a framework decision, to strengthen the fight against the aid for irregular entry and residence. we welcome the french presidency's reply and will examine this text carefully to see to what extent it can meet the objectives which the commission set itself in a proposal which it announced itself. two aspects should be given special attention in this context: the responsibility of employers and the consequences of employing illegal labour. it is not just a question of controlling the points of entry into the european union, it is also a question of controlling what is happening within our member states, especially with regard to the illegal labour market. on the other hand, trafficking in human beings must be specifically suppressed when it comes to the trafficking of women and children for the purpose of sexual exploitation, which is a specific aspect of trafficking in human beings in general. the commission recalls that the protection of victims and the respect of their rights must remain a constant concern in this legislative exercise. some victims pay with their lives, but others become slaves. in this connection, the commission intends to submit a proposal for the issue of short-term residence permits to victims who agree to cooperate with the police and judicial authorities in the fight against trafficking in human beings. indeed, the testimony of victims is essential for police and judicial prosecutions to produce effective results. however, in order to be able to rely on the testimony of victims, they must also be guaranteed a minimum of personal safety. the commission also invites the member states to strengthen their cooperation within europol, so that europol can make full use of all its resources to combat organised crime in this area. we stress in this connection the importance of the conference to be organized by the french presidency on 20 and 21 july in paris, of which the minister has just spoken. the schengen acquis as integrated by the treaty of amsterdam should also be developed and strengthened. in this regard, priority should be given to finding ways to ensure the proper application of existing law in the field of visa issuance and external border control by using the monitoring and control mechanisms available for this purpose. it will also be necessary to investigate existing arrangements, if necessary by making the necessary legislative amendments to the schengen acquis. it is necessary to look for all the means to facilitate administrative cooperation, such as the odysseus programme, where a third of the projects selected for the year 2000 already contribute to the fight against illegal immigration. the commission also shares the french presidency's desire to see the carrier liability regimes reviewed and adapted as a matter of priority. the commission takes note of the announcement by the presidency of an initiative in this regard. in our view, this initiative should be part of the development of the schengen acquis and adequately address the various means that can be used in close cooperation with operators because without this cooperation it will be difficult to combat human trafficking effectively. in the context of cooperation with countries and regions of origin and transit in general, particular attention should be paid to the fight against poverty and to respect for human rights. more specific actions could also be launched to develop information campaigns on the real possibilities of legal immigration and the prevention of all forms of trafficking, or to strengthen the capacity of the authorities of the countries of origin and transit to effectively combat this form of crime themselves. the possibility of increasing the appropriations for this purpose in the preliminary draft budget for 2001 should be considered. in addition, forms of enhanced cooperation could be envisaged for the applicant countries, in particular in the field of training and equipment, as well as in the field of exchange of information on the sectors. we believe that it is important to develop cooperation agreements between europol and the police forces of the applicant countries as quickly as possible. the conclusion and effective implementation of community readmission agreements will also help to reduce the attractiveness of offers from traffickers claiming to offer their victims guaranteed access to the territory of destination. more generally, it is necessary to address return policies as a whole, including assistance for voluntary return, in order to ensure their coordination and effectiveness while respecting the rights of the persons concerned. it is essential to make rapid progress towards the establishment of a common european asylum system, based on full respect for the right to asylum and the principle of non-refoulement, and aimed at offering, through fair and effective procedures, appropriate status to anyone in need of protection. the proposals already submitted by the commission on the european refugee fund and temporary protection, as well as the launch of the process of revising the dublin convention, should be followed up rapidly by other initiatives on procedures and conditions for the reception of asylum seekers. the commission is assessing the european parliament's report on its communication on the common procedure with a view to presenting a legislative initiative on a common asylum procedure in europe before the end of this year. we also welcome the french initiative to submit a guideline on the reception conditions for asylum seekers. the whole exercise is aimed, as was said in tampere, at achieving in the medium term a common definition of the concept of refugees and, in addition, other forms of subsidiary protection. the commission believes that any lasting solution requires the gradual introduction of a genuine immigration policy which, in line with the union's social, economic and demographic development prospects, ensures the admission of migrants in an orderly and clear framework and their harmonious integration into the host communities. a communication on this subject is being prepared by the commission in the autumn, as are legislative proposals which, following on from those on family reunification currently under consideration by the council, should define the conditions and procedures for admission for the purposes of employment and studies in particular. we all recognize the importance of this debate and we exchange views on the emotional impact of the dover tragedy. it is easy to say that the solution lies in more repressive measures. i too, as you have seen, am not giving up on the idea of strengthening repressive measures. but above all, we must understand that in the medium term, the success of this battle will be guaranteed by the formulation, at european level, of a proactive immigration policy, supported by clear criteria, shared by all member states. it must be a balanced policy, both in its aspect of 'integration of migrants' and in its aspect of 'effective combating of the organised crime networks' which exploit the victims of human trafficking. the balanced approach of the two strands of the european common policy in this respect must not be lost sight of at any time. human trafficking has become a bigger business than drug trafficking. organized crime has found that this trade is not only more profitable but also less risky. some 500,000 victims arrive in the european union each year. in the port of dover alone, hundreds of illegal immigrants are arrested every day. and with human trafficking, there is also the economic and sexual exploitation that is on the rise. the traffickers charge astronomical sums to get their clients to the west. once they arrive, they are often confiscated, and they are handed over to the criminal syndicates. the girls are regularly raped during the journey and then end up in the prostitution or illegal work circuit. and acts of terror and violence against victims who are unable to pay their debts are not uncommon. clearly, human traffickers are becoming more and more professional. they have better infrastructure and better communication and control facilities than the police or the legal services of the countries of origin or transit. this internationalisation of organised crime is in stark contrast to the inability of eu member states to adopt a coordinated approach to trafficking in human beings. everyone agrees that the problem is beyond the control, reception and repatriation capacities of the individual countries and that a common approach is needed. but despite all these good intentions, european cooperation is far from being up to the task. our police and judiciary are unable to shake off their nationalist reflexes. organized crime is of course taking advantage of the situation. if - (nl) mr president, ladies and gentlemen, the green group is largely satisfied with the council's common position. we feel that we have just made a great step forward. this is a real victory for this parliament. ladies and gentlemen, i would nevertheless like to draw attention briefly to the difference between traceability and consumer confidence. traceability, in my view, is technical information that can be entered and proved using numbers or barcodes that are illegible, impossible for the consumer to interpret. the key is that traceability is effective, not only to ensure consumer protection, but also to ensure the protection of the entire meat production and distribution chain. traceability makes it possible to identify the cause of the problem immediately when a crisis or scandal arises. this is to prevent the entire meat industry from being forced to destroy meat as a result of criminal acts or lack of caution. however, consumer confidence is not limited to traceability. i believe that confidence in a product can be strengthened by providing the consumer with reliable, but above all comprehensible information about the quality of the product he wishes to buy. it seems to me that indicating the category to which an animal belongs, provided that this is done in an understandable way, can help to increase consumer confidence. i therefore regret that a large majority of the committee on the environment, public health and consumer policy rejected this element and that the categorisation was finally removed. my group argued strongly at first reading for the breed and region of origin of the animal concerned to be indicated as well. i therefore regard it as regrettable that we did not win the race and the region at first reading. i would ask the meat industry not to be defensive on this issue, but rather to be proactive. i think it would be very useful for them and would also increase their turnover.

- i'd like to ask a question about the agenda. mr busquin has just made a statement on cloning. when will the debate take place?
- mr president, we are again faced with a fundamental ethical debate because of the evolution of biotechnology applied to humans. two opposing views are expressed, one of which rejects the exploitation of human beings, and in particular of embryos, and is concerned about the possible consequences for human society of the trivialization of certain techniques such as cloning. the other, which considers that the right of people with serious and incurable diseases to benefit from the potential of medical research is above all other considerations. the british government, in the absence of any prior international consultation, and i stress this, seems to have opted for the second approach, in favour of therapeutic cloning. the assumption underlying this decision is that therapeutic cloning, i.e. cloning undifferentiated embryonic cells from human embryos available for research and production, is a promising avenue. even if this hypothesis is well founded, the fact remains that this choice gives the human embryo the status of a stock of cells for medical use and leads to the production of embryos for research purposes first, and probably for production purposes later. i think it is important to make two points at this point. first of all, i would like to recall the council of europe convention on human rights and biomedicine, adopted in oviedo in april 1997. it may be inaccurate on a number of points, but it is very clear in article 18, which states: 'the constitution of human embryos for research purposes is prohibited'. there was a consensus in europe on this point until recently. the consensus has just been broken by the uk government's position. secondly, according to many experts, and as mr busquin, the research commissioner, has just mentioned, there are other ways of meeting the legitimate expectations of those who suffer from serious genetic diseases. this includes methods that do not require the production of embryos by cloning, but which use adult cells. why, then, in the face of this hypothesis, take an ethically and socially questionable leap forward? i think, ladies and gentlemen, that in conclusion, the knowledge gained in gene therapy can be promising for mankind, but it also carries risks and extreme deviations. we therefore need a strict legal framework and clear legal guidelines. the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for approval. it is the responsibility of our parliament to reaffirm this, not in haste, but simply by remaining consistent with previous positions.
mr president, at present the united kingdom has tax rates on fuel which are significantly higher than in other parts of the european union. this is having a devastating effect on the more remote parts of the country. it has a disproportionately distorting effect on competition, particularly affecting local businesses in peripheral areas which have to compete with large multinationals in the uk. that's an important caveat to consider when it comes to ways that fuel taxes can disrupt local economies. i do not think there is any good environmental reason for trying to cram the bulk of the uk population into the lower southwest corner. i didn't go into politics to depopulate the highlands again. that said, it is also downright idiotic to exempt aviation from the obligation to tackle greenhouse gas emissions and ozone depletion and it is also a good thing to explore the possibilities of a uniform taxation across the european union, as mrs lucas has done, rather than maintaining highly distorting differences between the different regions. i have my doubts about how this will play out in practice, but we must nevertheless embrace the principle, as mrs lucas has put it.

- mr president, if we are to proceed on the principle that 'an imperfect agreement is better than no agreement at all', then arusha has indeed been a step forward in the burundi peace process. all credit for this goes to mandela who spent months commuting to bring the various parties not only in speaking terms but also around the table. but the agreement is imperfect. there is no ceasefire yet and the fighting around the capital bujumbura continues. the two main hutu rebel movements in the army, which are responsible for most of the violence, were not involved in the negotiations. the next stage in nairobi, the meeting between the armed groups on 20 september, is therefore perhaps even more crucial than arusha. it is regrettable that the agreement does not mention the wider regional context. the neighbouring countries of the congo and tanzania, from which the armed groups operate, must also be reminded of their responsibility. the question arises, however, whether mandela has not overanalyzed the burundi conflict in his own south african context. it is correct that some tutsi parties use the danger of extermination of the minority as an alibi to maintain a supremacy. but that does not change the fact that this danger in burundi is a real one. and that is why i think we must demand adequate security guarantees for the minority and ensure that any elections do not incite ethnic hatred. finally, mr president, i think that europe should give the green light to the resumption of semi-structural aid, especially in the fields of education and health. if we do not, we risk being held responsible for the deaths of thousands of innocent civilians, and as a christian democrat i cannot accept that.
mr president, i can fully endorse the plea made by mr van den bos and mr van hecke, 200,000 dead in burundi. the commissioner has already said that the european parliament is not a political body. but i fear that the peace agreement came about because the heads of state needed such a peace agreement at the time. perhaps it would have taken a little longer to negotiate to get everyone around the table, because i am aware that as long as the main antagonists, both hutu and tutsi, are not around the table, there can be no real talk of peace. i am pleased that some of the movements which were once among the most irreconcilable have now been brought to the table. that is why i do not want to call this peace agreement a measure for nothing. i now ask myself how we as the european union can best respond to the wishes for peace that are coming from so many mouths in burundi. i think, first of all, that we will have to exert more pressure to achieve a global peace in the great lakes region. i cannot help feeling that there are still too many vested interests among the various african leaders to be able to pursue peace in the congo and peace in the great lakes region in a consistent manner. the second point is the famous resumption of aid. i have always regretted that the famous burundi boycott was brought about mainly because it was so selective. i hope that we can now go a long way with targeted peace projects, targeted democratic construction, which we support with structural aid.
mr president, commissioner, if you feel this current of misery coming over you every strasbourg partsession, you will wonder what we can really do. it is not just un soldiers, british soldiers, who are being held hostage in sierra leone, but a whole population has been held hostage there for years, and we have helped to do that, if only through the diamond trade. on 5 july the security council imposed an embargo on diamonds from sierra leone. we should now do everything we can to make this embargo a reality, to do everything we can on the eu side to put an end to all trade in blood diamonds. i therefore think that it is not enough to restrict the diamond trade in sierra leone. we must also continue to support the government, but i believe that support for the war tribunal is also absolutely necessary. we must put an end to the impunity with which people who take up arms can take the lives of their fellow citizens.

commission. – the release of the hostages on 13 july, the arrest of the rebel leader and his main associates on 26 july and the efforts of the government to reinstate law and order give some cause for satisfaction. however, there are still serious worries about the future of fiji. first and foremost, the use of violence by certain ethnic groupings to impose their rule on other communities is unacceptable. secondly, a democratic government must be re-established. the present interim government has no democratic basis. there should be a clear blue-print for full restoration of democratic principles and practices. the 1997 constitution, which lays down the principle of equal rights for all fijian citizens, must be reinstated. this process should be finalised very quickly. the time-frame currently being proposed, two or three years, is far too long. the fundamental ethnic antagonism in fiji must be resolved. multiracial cooperation and harmony must become a reality. this might require international mediation. if we are to be realistic, we must support every possible step on the way to stability without losing sight of the scenario i have just outlined. the international community and the donors of aid to fiji agree on these principles. as far as the eu is concerned, the council has informed the fijians of its request to begin consultations under article 366 of the lomé convention, which calls for a thorough examination of the situation on the basis of information to be supplied by the fijians. further input will be provided by the report of an acp ministerial fact-finding mission to fiji which took place in mid-august and which was led by the foreign minister of papua new guinea. furthermore, the commission received first-hand information from the legitimate prime minister, mr chaudhry on 31 august. we are now at the beginning of the consultations. measures under article 366 will be taken if the consultations fail to produce an acceptable solution for both sides in the discussions. all that can be said at present is that the measures, if any, should not harm the poor and those already suffering from the severe economic crisis triggered by the events. poverty-related projects should, consequently, continue. in conclusion, i hope that the coordinated effort of the international donor community will have the desired effect on the current government in fiji. even if we achieve our aim of restoring democratic principles and a government chosen in fair and equal elections within less than two years, this will be only a first step for the solution of the fijian problem. a strong long-term effort is required from all parties and, in the first instance, from the fijian government and the communities, to work on the entrenched prejudices and anxieties so that racial cooperation and harmony can be restored and become a reality in fiji. this is absolutely essential and there are no alternatives to that reality.
- mr president, what has happened in fiji in the last few months has been like something out of a soap opera. unfortunately, this was a bitter reality, and this in an acp country which enjoys considerable advantages under the sugar protocol with the european union. it is almost ironic that the signing of the new partnership agreement, which is a symbol of democracy, should take place in fiji at the very moment when a coup d'état was being staged there. it shows once again how fragile democracy is, even in those regions where it seemed to be an established value. but the fiji story is more complex! there are the delicate power relations between the indigenous population and the people of indian origin who make up 44% of the population. there's the questionable role of the military. the attacks on indian property seem to have been allowed to proceed unhindered without the intervention of law enforcement agencies, and elements within the military have openly sided with the coup. there are the recent revelations about speight's role in the lucrative timber industry that shed new light on the whole thing. recent information indicates that he was dismissed as chairman of the state-owned company that manages the mahogany plantations. this year, he threatened to seize a major contract for hardwood exploitation. all of this suggests that personal economic rather than political interests were at the heart of the coup. whatever the case, speight's undemocratic actions have plunged an entire country into a deep political and economic crisis. tourism, which accounts for one fifth of gross domestic product, has fallen to less than one third. ethnic tensions are rising again. thousands of people of indian origin are still on the run, and the events in fiji are also putting to the test the hopeful dynamic in the region that was to be crowned by the pacific regional trade agreement. as a parliament which in its agreements with the acp partners sets strict requirements for democracy and human rights, we cannot and must not accept such a course of action. we must continue to demand that the safety of all citizens be guaranteed and that any form of ethnic division and incitement be punished. speight can't walk free. we must above all call for a return to genuine democracy under the 1997 constitution which guarantees equal rights to all fijians and indeed elections as soon as possible. a transitional period of three years is unacceptable. if the interim government fails to ensure a return to democracy within a reasonable period of time, sanctions should be considered, preferably smart sanctions, and structural aid should also be suspended. the consultations on this will begin in september. i hope that the commission will stick to its principles and not give in to political or economic pressure. as we stand at the beginning of a new major partnership agreement between europe and its acp partners, it is of the utmost importance that no precedent is set.
- (fr) mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the coup d'état in fiji in may of this year was not the first, there was also a coup d'état in 1987 which resulted in expulsion from the commonwealth, and that reminds me that the tensions, the apparent ethnic tensions, must be much deeper than we can possibly imagine. again, the tension was between indigenous fijians and indo-fijians, and of course 44% against the indigenous, 64%, which are two almost proportional populations. i can imagine that what you have said, commissioner, about the elimination of prejudice, is not self-evident in such situations. unemployment is also fuelling tensions and the legally elected government was ousted by that military coup. what worries me even more is that this conflict could spill over to other islands where similar problems and similar tensions are occurring. in fact, it is the opposite of what we are aiming at and what has already been alluded to: the creation of a major pacific regional trade agreement between 14 nations. if we are not careful, this will be the first major conflict in the pacific and it could affect the situation in the region for decades to come. all of this makes me ask the question: isn't this a coup d'etat foreshadowing something worse? and it also makes me ask the question: what is the european union's policy on prevention? what steps are being taken to put such a country on the road to a democratic state of law, away from the state of emergency, the trial of the guilty, as rightly requested, security for all citizens, that seems to me to be elementary, and that in a country where thousands of people are refugees in their own country; the european parliament is not the only institution which has to be consulted on the matter. some of our resolutions said: the european parliament has been asked to vote on the report by mr glinne, and i am sure that the commission will be able to do so. commissioner, the european union is fiji's most important trading partner and it is also its most important donor. how can we now translate this role into effective peace-making? could we not appoint a real mediator or a mediation body in consultation with the un and the commonwealth? the cotonou agreements provide for a partnership for development and peace, and the lomé agreements also provide for procedures, particularly in article 366, to which reference has already been made. these procedures should be applied with care, in a prudent but not naive manner. it would be good if the commission came back to us before the end of the year and reported to parliament on developments in fiji and in the whole region and on the efforts made by the european union.

mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, mrs ferrer's report deals with the commission's communication on the complementarity of community and member states' development cooperation. with her, i regret that we're treating this separately. in fact, we should have a thorough debate on a global strategy for our cooperation with the countries of the south with the triangle: the commission alibi which the member states are gratefully using to prevent it from becoming complementary and certainly not coordinating and cohesive. some member states are quite frankly of the opinion that the community should not pursue a development policy and that they should leave everything to the member states. we do not share this view at all. anyone who is genuinely concerned with just world relations in the field of development should admit that it is not the relevance of development that is decisive for the policies of the member states, but very often their own strategic and commercial interests. at best, they try to mitigate or cure the effects of economic policies that are being induced from the rich countries in this case globalisation, our need for raw materials, minerals, oil, and so on. we certainly want the poor countries to be included in the general economic and cultural developments in the world in the interests of the people concerned and not just in the interests of the industrialized world. we must therefore abandon these neo-colonial relations and those which maintain dependence and destroy the environment. we must end the poverty in the world. this requires a global multilateral strategy. this requires first and foremost coordination of the development policies of the member states and the community, in each country, in each recipient country and at international level. we believe that this is more important than complementarity. there is no doubt that a more coordinated policy would have a much greater impact. the discrepancy between the high amount of our european aid, more than half of all the aid in the world, and the low political impact, for example to stop gross violations of human rights, to stop armed conflicts, is really painful. if coordination and complementarity were to take place in the recipient countries, the donor countries would prefer to fly a national flag there too. is the council really prepared to change the comitology system? i think that is a conditio sine qua non. delegating eu responsibilities to our delegations on the ground is a serious challenge for the commission. if this does not succeed, it is certain that the opponents of the common development policy will insist on its abolition. we want a european approach to international coordination which may differ from the approach of the imf and the world bank. we do not want to be a pawn in the bretton woods institutions. we want to travel the world. that is why those member states must tell their voters something other than that europe is simply going wrong. they must urgently acknowledge their own part in these failures. but we have to do our part.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by saying that my colleague mr wallis has made every effort to bring this report to the table. the report as adopted in committee is, in my view, an example of a balance between the interests of all parties, both small and mediumsized enterprises and consumers. it would be a pity if the balance were to be disturbed by tomorrow's vote, and i can hardly believe that the council will be able to agree to the amendments before us. let me explain why. the terminology as it stands today is clear. we are talking about contracts concluded at a distance, a terminology which is much clearer even to a layman than directed activities, as is now proposed in the amendment. let me illustrate this again with an example. a french winemaker is advertising his cremant in french. a french consumer will enjoy the protection of the brussels convention because the activities of the winegrower are purposely directed in a substantial way, but the english, belgian and italian consumers will not enjoy protection because the activities were not directed directly and intentionally at these consumers. the result will be a great deal of legal uncertainty and a variety of court cases where it will be necessary to examine on a case-by-case basis whether the activities were deliberate or not. secondly, i think it is very important that the report should provide for the possibility of the parties themselves to include a jurisdiction clause in their contract. freedom of contract is a fundamental concept. if contractual freedom and therefore the constitutional right of a party to go to court is denied, i foresee various procedures that will be initiated for refusal of justice. i think that the brussels convention is strategically very important for the development of electronic commerce within the internal market and that it will also have important implications for the success or failure of small and mediumsized enterprises. i therefore hope that this report, as adopted in committee, will be adopted unchanged in the plenary tomorrow.
- mr president, on 21 september this year, in the commune of de panne, which is in the extreme west of my country, close to the french border, a farmer found 45 refugees in his field one evening, and among those 45 refugees were 15 children. these people were taken in and cared for by that municipality, but the strange thing was that they claimed to have been put across the border by the french police. the police had arrested them in calais, taken them from calais to the belgian border and then quietly dropped them off in belgium, a very strange case, especially since other testimonies show that this is not the first time this has happened. fortunately, the matter has been discussed at the highest level between france and belgium, and it seems that a way has been found to discuss it. i was surprised, however, when a belgian who had given me information told me that it was not only the french who were doing this, but also the dutch and the germans. when i asked him if the belgians did that, he said yes, occasionally. so it seems that everyone still has the correct european reflex, namely, we take our problem across the border to the others. i think, madam president, that it is urgent that we, as european regulators, as the european parliament, should at least insist on a european reflex here. the commission has not yet taken a decision on the matter, and i am not sure that it will be possible to do so.

and mr moscovici has made this clear - the igc does not unblock the situation, particularly with regard to the extension of the qualified majority, which implies the abandonment of the right of veto and the generalization of co-decision, it will be a failure and we will have to continue. i believe that postponement would be the worst choice for european citizens and for citizens of the candidate countries. i do not have time to go into detail on enlargement, but i would like to conclude, madam president, by stressing another aspect of enlargement: we are asking the applicant countries to adapt their institutions, their economies and their legislation, but we must also adapt our policies. the main challenge we face is to prevent the european union from being just a large market of 500 million consumers with major structural imbalances, and to do this i believe we should launch the constitutional process in nice which would allow for this profound reform of the institutions, this reform in the hierarchy of values and which would finally put free trade somewhere else than in the first place. the committee on economic and monetary affairs and industrial policy has adopted a number of amendments to the proposal. - mr president, commissioner, social policy is a cornerstone of the european social model. it is also a productive factor contributing to economic development, and although the candidate countries have already made considerable efforts in this regard, much remains to be done to reduce the development gap between the european union and the candidate countries to acceptable proportions. i'll give you a few examples: their wages are four times lower than ours, their purchasing power is two and a half times lower than ours; only prague and bratislava have the highest average prosperity in the european union. compared with the european union, labour market participation in the central and eastern european countries is about 5% lower, for women about 20%. the infant mortality rate in eastern europe is twice that of western europe. in romania, child mortality is four times higher than in the european union. the east and the west are still distinguished by their technological advances: there are four internet connections per thousand inhabitants in their country, and there are 16 in ours. if there is talk of rapid accession - and the date 2003 is also the case, although the commissioner does not want to commit himself to that, but does not take into account the fact that some western european heads of government are also proposing that date - then this may presuppose transitional measures or periods, and there is still uncertainty about this. for example, what procedure will be used to decide on these transitional periods? if the acquis communautaire is not or cannot be fully transposed or has not been applied in practice, how is it to be decided what part of the acquis communautaire must be transposed in any case and for what part, subject to examination and interpretation, can a transitional period be accepted if necessary?
